# 107. Spin-off Companies

## 1. Indicator Overview

- **Code:** IV2

- **Maximum Points:** 30

- **Definition:** Measures the number of spin-off companies/foundations created by faculty, staff, or alumni in the last 5 years that are still active and independent (no longer relying on university financial/administrative support).

- **Purpose:** Recognises institutions that foster entrepreneurship, technology transfer, and commercialization of research/ideas.

## 2. Scope & Inclusions

## ‘What counts as Spin-offs:

- Companies legally registered by faculty, students, staff, or alumni based on intellectual property, research output, or entrepreneurial initiative developed at the institution.

- Foundations or not-for-profit entities addressing social/technological challenges.

- Start-ups incubated in the university incubation centre that have graduated and are self-sustaining.

- **Joint ventures initiated during the last 5 years but now operating independently. What is excluded:**

- Subsidiaries of the university still under its financial control.

- Student clubs or informal entrepreneurship projects without legal registration.

- Companies dissolved, merged, or inactive.

- Start-ups still dependent on university financial aid or space (not graduated).

## 3. Measurement & Formula

- **Benchmark:** 5 spin-off companies (scaled down).

- **Scoring Formula:**

Points=min (30,No. of eligible spin-offs/5×30)

- **Example:**

| **Criterion / condition** | **Points / scoring**         |
|---------------------------|------------------------------|
| If 5+ spinoffs            | Full 30/30 points.           |
| If 3 spinoffs             | (3 + 5 × 30) = 18/30 points. |
| If 1 spinoff              | (1 + 5 x 30) = 6/30 points.  |

## 4. Evidence Requirements

- List of registered spin-offs with incorporation details (CIN/LLP No. etc.).

- Proof of founders’ affiliation with the institution (faculty ID, alumni record).

- Documentation of university-originated IP/technology licensed to spin-off (if applicable).

- Business registration certificates, GST number, or ROC filings.

- Confirmation of financial/operational independence (not subsidised by the university).

- Website or annual report link of the spin-off company.

## 5. Example Scenarios

- **STEM Institution:**

| **Criterion / condition**                                                                      | **Points / scoring** |
|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| A biotech incubator supports 6 start-ups in the last 5 years; 5 have graduated and are active. | Full 30 points.      |

- **Non-STEM Institution:**

| **Criterion / condition**                                                               | **Points / scoring**      |
|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------|
| 2 spin-offs in social entrepreneurship (NGO + consultancy) are operating independently. | (2 + 5 x 30) = 12 points. |

- **Mixed Institution:**

| **Criterion / condition**                                | **Points / scoring**      |
|----------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------|
| 4 spinoffs (3 tech + 1 NGO) still running independently. | (4 + 5 × 30) = 24 points. |

## 6. Best Practices to Maximise Score

- Establish Technology Transfer & Entrepreneurship Cells to support spin-off creation.

- Provide incubation services but ensure companies graduate within 3–5 years.

- Develop clear spin-off and IP commercialization policies.

- Track alumni entrepreneurs through alumni networks & entrepreneurship cells.

- Encourage faculty-industry collaborations that can evolve into spin-offs. Spin-off Companies — Tracker

## Purpose:

- Maintain structured records of institutional spin-offs/start-ups for accreditation, audits, rankings, and innovation ecosystem reporting.

## How to use:

1\) Enter one row per spin-off company in SpinOff_Data.

2\) Capture key details (founder, department, sector, incorporation date, funding, jobs created, revenue).

3\) Use dropdowns for Sector, Status, Currency, Proof Type.

4\) Link evidence (MoU, registration certificate, funding proof, annual reports) in Proof URL.

5\) Use Summary sheet for KPIs and Revenue_Matrix for year × sector analysis.

## Key definitions:

- **Status:** Active, Acquired, Closed.

- **Sector:** Tech, Healthcare, Agri, Manufacturing, Services, Other.

- **Proof:** MoU, Registration Certificate, Funding Proof, Annual Report, Other.

- **Revenue:** Annual revenue reported by the spin-off (in chosen currency).

- **Jobs Created:** Direct jobs generated by the company since inception. *Tip:* Use *filters* in SpinOff_Data to view *active* vs *closed* companies, or revenue growth *trends.*

# 108. External Consultations

## 1. Indicator Overview

- **Code:** 1V3

- **Maximum Points:** 20

- **Definition:** Measures the proportion of faculty members who hold a parallel appointment/engagement in non-academic roles (industry, government, NGOs, think tanks, policy boards, etc.).

- **Purpose:** Recognises institutions that encourage knowledge transfer, industry collaboration, and policy engagement by leveraging faculty expertise outside academia.

## 2. Scope & Inclusions

## ‘What counts as external consultation appointments:

- Faculty serving as consultants/advisors to industries (short– or long-term contracts).

- Faculty members with honorary/part-time positions in NGOs, think tanks, or foundations.

- Membership in government committees, advisory boards, or standard-setting bodies.

- Involvement in professional associations or policy task forces linked to expertise.

- Parallel appointments in R&D labs or international organizations.

## What is excluded:

- Purely voluntary or non-professional community service.

- Temporary one-day workshops/guest talks (not formal appointments).

- Paid teaching roles in other universities (academic, not non-academic).

- Unverified /unofficial consulting activities without contracts or appointments.

## 3. Measurement & Formula

- **Benchmark:** 5% of total faculty with qualifying external appointments = Full 20 points.

- **Formula:**

Points=min(20,(Faculty with external consultation + Total Faculty) × 100/5x20)

- **Example Calculations:**

  - Total faculty = 200, with 15 having external appointments → (15 + 200 × 100 = 7.5%).

| **Criterion / condition**                                    | **Points / scoring**                  |
|--------------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------|
| Since 7.5% ≥ 5%,                                             | 20/20 points.                         |
| **Total faculty = 100, with 2 having external appointments** | **(2%). (2 + 5 × 20) = 8/20 points.** |

## 4. Evidence Requirements

- Verified list of faculty with external non-academic appointments.

- Appointment letters or MoUs with industries/NGOs/government.

- Proof of active consultancy engagement (contract duration, role description).

- Policy documents enabling faculty-industry/government collaborations.

- Faculty self-declaration with institutional approval for compliance.

## 5. Example Scenarios

| **Criterion / condition**                                                                                            | **Points / scoring**                    |
|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------|
| **STEM Institution:** 12 faculty members out of 180 serve as consultants to tech companies and government R&D boards | (12 + 180 = 6.7%) → 20/20 points.       |
| **Non-STEM Institution:** 4 faculty out of 120 are members of social policy think tanks and NGOs                     | (3.3%) → (3.3 + 5 × 20) = 13/20 points. |
| **Mixed Institution:** 10 faculty out of 300 serve in government committees, industry forums, and NGOs               | (3.3%) → 13/20 points.                  |

## 6. Best Practices to Maximise Score

- Establish an industry-academia interface office to facilitate faculty consulting.

- Encourage faculty to take up government/NGO committee roles.

- Formalise policies for consultancy workload sharing to avoid teaching conflict.

- Maintain a consultancy register updated annually with supporting documents.

- Showcase consultancy cases in annual reports and on the institutional website for visibility.

External Consultations — Tracker

## Purpose:

- Maintain structured records of external consultancy projects for accreditation, audits, rankings, and internal reporting.

## How to use:

1\) Enter one row per consultancy project in Consult_Data.

2\) Capture key details (client, department, PI, dates, revenue, status).

3\) Use dropdowns for Consultancy Type, Status, Funding Source, Currency, Proof Type. 4) Link evidence (MoU, agreement, invoice, completion report) in Proof URL. 5) Use

## Summary sheet for KPIs and Revenue_Matrix for year × department analysis. Key definitions:

- **Consultancy Type:** Technical, Policy, Training, Advisory, Other.

- **Status:** Ongoing, Completed, Terminated.

- **Funding Source:** Government, Industry, NGO, International, Other.

- **Revenue Currency:** Default (can adapt to USD/EUR).

- **Proof:** Agreement, Invoice, Report, Other.

***Tip:*** Use *filters* in Consult_Data to create client- or year-specific *summaries.*

# 109. Commercialisation of Intellectual Property

## 1. Indicator Overview

- **Code:** V4

- **Maximum Points:** 10

- **Definition:** Measures the percentage of institutional revenue derived from royalty income earned through commercialisation of intellectual property (IP) such as patents, copyrights, trademarks, or technology transfers.

- **Purpose:** Recognises institutions that successfully convert research and innovation into revenue-generating assets, demonstrating real-world application and industry relevance.

## 2. Scope & Inclusions

## ‘What counts as Royalty Income:

- Licensing fees received for patents or copyrighted works.

- Revenue from technology transfer agreements.

- Royalties from software, digital products, publications, or creative works.

- Payments from trademark/brand usage agreements.

- **Income from spin-offs or startups where royalty-sharing is part of IP agreements. What is excluded:**

- Tuition fees or student charges.

- General donations and endowments (not linked to IP).

- Consultancy income (covered in IV3).

- Government/industry project grants (counted under RE4 External Funding).

## 3. Measurement & Formula

- **Benchmark:** 10% of institutional revenue from royalty = Full 10 points.

## 4. Scoring Examples

- **Example 1:**

  - Total institutional revenue = 100 Cr

  - Royalty earned = 312 Cr

| **Criterion / condition** | **Points / scoring**           |
|---------------------------|--------------------------------|
| Royalty % = 12%           | ≥ 10% threshold → 10/10 points |

- **Example 2:**

  - Revenue = 380 Cr

  - Royalty earned = 34 Cr

| **Criterion / condition** | **Points / scoring**        |
|---------------------------|-----------------------------|
| Royalty % = 5%            | (5 + 10 × 10) = 5/10 points |

- **Example 3:**

  - Revenue = 350 Cr

  - Royalty earned = ¥1 Cr

| **Criterion / condition** | **Points / scoring**        |
|---------------------------|-----------------------------|
| Royalty % = 2%            | (2 + 10 × 10) = 2/10 points |

## 5. Evidence Requirements

- Audited financial statements showing total revenue and royalty income separately.

- Licensing/royalty agreements with industries, publishers, or commercial partners.

- Technology transfer office (TTO) records.

- nnual report with IP revenue disclosure.

## 6. Best Practices to Maximise Score

- Establish a Technology Transfer & Licensing Office (TTLO).

- Proactively seek industry partnerships for IP licensing.

- Support faculty and student innovations with patent filing and market linkages.

- Negotiate revenue-sharing agreements with startups/spin-offs.

- Regularly review and update the institutional IP & Royalty Policy.

Commercialisation of Intellectual Property — Tracker

## Purpose:

- Maintain structured records of IP assets, licensing, spin-offs, and revenue for accreditation, audits, rankings, and internal monitoring.

## How to use:

1\) Enter one row per IP asset or commercialisation event in IP_Data.

2\) Capture key metadata (type, inventor, filed/granted dates, licensing details, revenue). 3) Use dropdowns for IP Type, Status, Commercialisation Mode, Revenue Currency, Proof Type.

4\) Link evidence documents (patents, MoUs, license agreements, revenue receipts) in Proof URL.

5\) Use Summary sheet for KPIs and Revenue_Matrix for year × revenue type reporting.

## Key definitions:

- **IP Type:** Patent, Copyright, Trademark, Design, Trade Secret, Other.

- **Status:** Filed, Granted, Licensed, Commercialised, Expired.

- **Commercialisation Mode:** License, Technology Transfer, Spin-off/Startup, Assignment, Consultancy.

- **Revenue Currency:** Default ¥ (can adapt to USD/EUR if required).

- **Proof:** Patent doc, License Agreement, MoU, Revenue Invoice, Other. *Tip:* *Filters* in IP_Data *enable* easy year-wise and IP-type-wise analysis.

# 110. \[ADVANCED\] Internationalisation (IS)

## 1. Purpose

- Evaluates the institution’s ability to integrate global dimensions into its academic, cultural, and research activities.

- Encourages partnerships, mobility, and exposure to international best practices.

- Measures how far the campus fosters a globally competitive learning environment.

## 2. Indicator Breakdown

### IS1 – International Students (30 points)

- **Definition:** Percentage of enrolled students holding non-domestic nationality.

- **Scoring Rule:**

  - 10 points per 1% of international students.

  - Max 30 points (3% or more of student population).

- **Purpose:** Captures diversity, cultural exchange, and global presence. IS2 — Student *Exchange* Programmes (20 points)

- **Sub-indicators:**

| **Criterion / condition**                                                                                          | **Points / scoring** |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| **(i) Inbound mobility:** Students from abroad studying ≥2 weeks. - Benchmark: 2% of student population            | 10 points.           |
| **(ii) Outbound mobility:** Institution’s students studying abroad ≥2 weeks. - Benchmark: 2% of student population | 10 points.           |

- **Purpose:** Tracks institutional commitment to two-way student mobility. IS3 – *International* Faculty *Exchange* (20 points)

- **Sub-indicators:**

  - **(i) Inbound Faculty:** Faculty from international partner universities visiting ≥2 weeks.

| **Criterion / condition**               | **Points / scoring** |
|-----------------------------------------|----------------------|
| **Benchmark:** 1% of faculty population | 15 points.           |

- **(ii) Outbound Faculty:** Institution’s faculty working in international partner universities (≥2 weeks, not self-funded).

| **Criterion / condition** | **Points / scoring** |
|---------------------------|----------------------|
| **Benchmark:** 3%         | 5 points.            |

- **Purpose:** Measures academic knowledge transfer and global networking. IS4 — *International* Relations/Exchange *Office* (10 points)

- **Scoring:**

| **Criterion / condition** | **Points / scoring**                                                                                                |
|---------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| 5 points                  | Formal policies establishing an International Office. o 5 points → Presence of full-time staff managing operations. |

- **Purpose:** Institutionalisation of support structures for internationalisation. IS5 — *International* *Collaborations* (20 points)

- **5 points each for MOUs/MOAs signed in the last 24 months that result in:** » (i) Joint Conferences

  - \(ii\) Joint Research Projects

  - \(iii\) Dual Degrees / Semester Exchange

  - \(iv\) Summer/ Winter Schools

- **Purpose:** Captures active partnerships beyond paper agreements.

## 3. Scoring Summary

Code Indicator

181 International Students

1S2 Student Exchange Programmes Max Benchmarkpoints

30 3% of student population

20 2% inbound + 2% outbound Code IndicatorMax Benchmarkpoints

1s3 International Faculty (Inbound & 5 19 inbound, 3% outboundOutbound)

1S4 International Office 10 Policy + Full-time staff

4 active outcomes in last 24 IS5 International Collaborations 20 months

Total 100

## 4. Evidence Required

- Student enrolment reports (with nationality breakdown).

- Exchange programme records, MoUs, and certificates of participation.

- Faculty deployment and visiting records.

- Policy documents establishing International Office + HR contracts.

- MOUs/MOAs + proof of activities (conference reports, research publications, curriculum agreements).

## 5. Best Practices for Institutions

- Establish a centralised International Office with dedicated staff.

- Proactively partner with top 500 global universities.

- Integrate short-term mobility (summer schools, virtual exchange).

- Create joint research labs with international collaborators.

- Develop dual degree programmes to attract global student inflows.

# 111. International Students

## 1. Indicator Overview

- **Code:** 181

- **Max Points:** 30

- **Definition:** Measures the proportion of enrolled international students compared to the total student population.

- **Scoring:** 10 points per 1% international students, capped at 30 points ≥3%).

## 2. Purpose

- Encourages institutions to attract and retain international students.

- Promotes cultural diversity and global campus exposure.

- Reflects the international reputation and competitiveness of the institution.

## 3. Scoring Thresholds

% of International Students Points Awarded

0%0

0.5% 5

1% 10

2% 20

39% or more 30 (full)

## 4. Evidence Requirements

- Student enrolment records (with nationality column).

- Passport/visa/immigration documents for international student verification.

- Institutional database (ERP/MIS) student demographic reports.

- Audited headcount reports submitted to government agencies (if applicable).

## 5. Example Scenarios

| **Criterion / condition**                                        | **Points / scoring**          |
|------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------|
| **Case 1:** Total students = 10,000; International students = 50 | 0.5% → Score = 5 points.      |
| **Case 2:** Total students = 8,000; International students = 240 | 3% → Score = 30 points (max). |
| **Case 3:** Total students = 5,000; International students = 100 | 2% → Score = 20 points.       |

International Students — Tracker

## Purpose:

- Maintain structured records of international students for accreditation, rankings, audits, and website disclosure.

## How to use:

1\) Enter one row per student in Intl_Data (covering admission, demographics, programme, fees, support).

2\) Use dropdowns for Gender, Level, Mode, Scholarship, Visa, Insurance, etc. 3) Track both fee-paying and exchange/short-term students (specify in Programme Type). 4) Attach evidence links in Proof URL (offer letters, passports, fee receipts, etc.). 5) Use Summary sheet for KPIs and Country_Matrix for year x country reporting. Key definitions:

- **Level:** UG / PG / PhD.

- **Mode:** Full-time / Part-time.

- **Programme Type:** Regular Degree / Exchange / Short-term / Dual Degree.

- **Status:** Enrolled / Graduated / Withdrawn.

- **Fees:** Record tuition + other charges in local currency (3).

- **Scholarship:** Yes = any tuition waiver/financial aid.

***Tip:*** *Filters* in Intl_Data *enable* easy year-wise and country-wise breakdowns.

# 112. Student Exchange Programmes

## 1. Indicator Overview

- **Code:** 1S2

- **Max Points:** 20

- **Definition:** Measures the proportion of students participating in inbound (foreign students hosted) and outbound (domestic students sent abroad) exchange programmes lasting at least 2 weeks with partnered institutions.

- **Scoring:**

| **Criterion / condition** | **Points / scoring**                                                                                                                   |
|---------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| 10 points                 | Inbound exchange students (≥2% of total student population). 10 points → Outbound exchange students (≥2% of total student population). |

## 2. Purpose

- Promotes international mobility of students.

- Ensures cultural immersion and academic collaboration.

- Enhances the institution’s global brand and partnerships.

- Encourages reciprocal exchange rather than one-way mobility.

## 3. Formula for Calculation

Points Awarded = Inbound Points + Outbound Points

- Inbound Points = MIN(10, (Inbound% ÷ 2%) × 10)

- Outbound Points = MIN(10, (Outbound% + 2%) × 10)

## 4. Scoring Thresholds

% of Students (Inbound/Outbound) Points (Each)

0%0

0.5% 2.5

1% 5

1.5%7.5

29% or more 10 (full)

Total Maximum = 20 points.

## 5. Evidence Requirements

- MoUs/MoAs with partnered international institutions.

- Student records (enrolment & participation lists).

- Duration proof (exchange programme letters, visa/stay records, or certificates).

- Exchange office/International Office reports.

- Photographs and testimonials (for qualitative validation).

## 6. Example Scenarios

## Case 1–Inbound only:

- Total students = 5,000

| **Criterion / condition**      | **Points / scoring** |
|--------------------------------|----------------------|
| Inbound exchange students = 50 | 1% → 5 points        |
| Outbound exchange students = 0 | 0 points             |

- Total = 5/20 points

## Case 2 — Balanced mobility:

- Total students = 10,000

| **Criterion / condition** | **Points / scoring** |
|---------------------------|----------------------|
| Inbound = 250 (2.5%)      | 10 points            |
| Outbound = 150 (1.5%)     | 7.5 points           |

- Total = 17.5/20 points

## Case 3 — Strong outbound focus:

- Total students = 8,000

| **Criterion / condition** | **Points / scoring** |
|---------------------------|----------------------|
| Inbound = 40 (0.5%)       | 2.5 points           |
| Outbound = 240 (3%)       | 10 points            |

- Total = 12.5/20 points

Student Exchange Programmes — Tracker (Outgoing & Incoming Mobility)

## Purpose:

- Maintain auditable records of all mobility (semester/short-term/research/dual-degree) for accreditation, rankings, and website disclosure.

## How to use:

1\) Add/update partner information in the Partners sheet (MoU status, dates, coordinators).

2\) Enter each mobility in Exchange_Data (one row per student per mobility). 3) Use drop-downs for Direction, Level, Mobility Type, Funding, Status, etc. 4) Attach evidence links (nomination, acceptance, transcripts, grant letters) in Proof URL columns.

## 5) View the Summary dashboard and Mobility_Matrix for counts by year & partner. Key definitions:

- **Direction:** Outgoing (our students going out) / Incoming (visiting students hosted by us).

- **Mobility Type:** Semester/Year, Short-term (≤8 weeks), Summer/ Winter School, Research Internship, Dual Degree.

- **Credits:** Use ECTS or map to local credit system; record both if applicable.

- **Status:** Planned (nominated), Accepted (confirmed), Completed (transcript received), Withdrawn (cancelled).

***Tip:*** Use data *filters* in Exchange_Data and slicers/pivots (optional) for ad-hoc analytics.

incoming student mobility (semester/year, short-term, summer/winter school, research internship, dual degree).

- Designed for accreditation/rankings (QS/NAAC), audits, and website disclosure with evidence links.

## What’s included (sheets)

- README – usage steps, definitions, quick tips.

- Partners – partner directory (country, city, disciplines, coordinator, MoU ID, dates, active Yes/No, notes).

- Exchange_Data – structured records with dropdowns & formulas (one row per mobility).

- Summary – KPIs, breakdowns (direction, top partners), country counts, charts.

- Mobility_Matrix — partner x academic year counts with auto totals.

## Key fields captured (Exchange_Data)

- Academic Year, Term (dropdown), Direction (Outgoing/Incoming), Mobility Type, Status (Planned/Accepted/Completed/ Withdrawn), Level (UG/PG/PhD).

- Student/ Visitor ID, Name, Gender (dropdown), Home Institution/Dept, Host Partner, Host Country, Host Dept.

- Credits (Home & ECTS), Funding Source, Grant Amount (3).

- **Evidence:** Proof Type & Proof URL.

## Data validations (built-in)

- Standardized lists for Direction, Level, Term, Mobility Type, Funding Source, Status, Gender, Yes/No/N/A, Proof Type.

- Prevents inconsistent entries; improves audit-readiness.

## Auto-metrics (Summary)

- Total/Outgoing/Incoming mobilities; Completed count.

- Unique partners, total & average grant, total ECTS, average duration.

- Female participants count.

- **Rows:** partners pulled from Partners sheet (first 30 by default).

- **Columns:** editable academic years (8-year window; counts update automatically).

## Partner registry (compliance-ready)

- MoU metadata (ID, validity dates, Active Yes/No), coordinator contact, website.

- Facilitates quick checks for active MoU before approving mobility.

## Starter data

- Example partners (TU Berlin, University of Melbourne) and one completed outgoing record (editable/replaceable).

- Duration formula pre-set; grant column uses currency format.

- Extend lists (countries, funding schemes) in hidden \_lists; validations update automatically.

- Add filters or PivotTables (optional) for ad-hoc analytics; website disclosure can mirror

Summary KPIs.

# 113. Inbound & Outbound International Faculty

## 1. Indicator Overview

- **Code:** IS3

- **Max Points:** 20

- **Definition:** Evaluates the mobility of faculty between the host institution and partnered international universities.

- **Two Components:**

  - **Inbound Faculty (15 points):** Foreign faculty working at the host institution for at least 2 weeks.

  - **Outbound Faculty (5 points):** Host institution’s faculty working at partnered universities abroad for at least 2 weeks (not self-funded).

## 2. Purpose

- Promotes knowledge transfer and cross-border academic collaboration.

- Encourages international research partnerships.

- Enhances teaching quality by integrating global best practices.

- Improves institution’s global standing in rankings and accreditation.

## 3. Formula for Calculation

- Inbound Score = MIN(15, (Inbound % + 1%) x 15)

- Outbound Score = MIN(5, (Outbound % + 3%) × 5)

Total IS3 Score = Inbound Score + Outbound Score (max 20)

## 4. Scoring Thresholds

Component % Threshold Full Points

Inbound Faculty 1% of total faculty 15 points Outbound Faculty 3% of total faculty 5 points

## 5. Evidence Requirements

Scaling Logic

Proportional scaling up to 1% Proportional scaling up to 3%

- Faculty exchange agreements with partner institutions.

- Appointment/secondment letters (with duration).

- Salary/financial support documents (to prove not self-funded for outbound).

- Faculty travel and visa records (supporting stay duration).

- Institutional HR/International Office reports.

## 6. Example Scenarios

## Case 1 – Strong inbound focus:

- Total faculty = 500

- Inbound faculty = 10 → (10 + 500) × 100 = 2% → Full 15/15

- Outbound faculty = 5 → (5 ÷ 500) × 100 = 1% → (1 ÷ 3) × 5 = 1.67/5

- Total = 16.67/20

## Case 2 – Balanced inbound & outbound:

- Total faculty = 400

- Inbound =4 → 1% → Full15/15

- Outbound = 12 → 3% → Full 5/5

- Total = 20/20

## Case 3 —- Weak international mobility:

- Total faculty = 200

- Inbound = 1 → 0.5% → (0.5 ÷ 1) × 15 = 7.5/15

- Outbound = 1 → 0.5% → (0.5 + 3) x 5 =0.83/5

- Total = 8.33/20

# 114. International Student/Relations/Exchange Office (ISREO) Policy

## 1. Purpose

- To foster internationalisation of the institution through structured policies, programmes, and partnerships.

- To provide a dedicated support mechanism for inbound and outbound students, faculty, and collaborations.

- To align with QS I-GAUGE Internationalisation (IS4) requirements for compliance and accreditation.

## 2. Scope

- Applies to all international students (inbound/outbound), exchange participants, and faculty mobility programmes.

- Covers policy, staffing, administration, and operational aspects of internationalisation.

## 3. Policy Statement

- The institution shall maintain an International Student/Relations/Exchange Office (ISREO) as the central body for internationalisation activities.

- The ISREO shall have full-time professional staff and clear documented policies.

- The office will coordinate exchange programmes, global partnerships, student/faculty mobility, and cross-border initiatives.

## 4. Objectives

- To increase the number of international students and faculty on campus.

- To enhance outbound mobility of students and faculty.

- To facilitate joint conferences, dual degrees, summer/winter schools, and collaborative research with international partners.

- To ensure compliance with national/international regulations for visas, admissions, and partnerships.

## 5. Governance & Responsibilities

- Director, ISREO – Strategic leadership, reporting to Vice-Chancellor.

- International Officer(s) - Administration of exchange agreements, student services, visa assistance.

- Student Liaison Officer — Support for cultural integration, orientation programmes, grievance redressal.

- Faculty Mobility Coordinator —- Management of inbound/outbound faculty programmes.

## 6. Key Functions

- Develop and maintain MOUs/MOAs with foreign universities.

- Administer student/faculty exchange programmes.

- Conduct orientation programmes for international students.

- Provide visa guidance, housing support, and emergency support services.

- Ensure periodic review of internationalisation metrics.

## 7. Compliance Table (QS I-GAUGE Alignment)

1S4 Requirement

Policy formulated to establish ISREO

Presence of full-time staff at ISREO

Total Score (Max 10)

Evidence Status Points Approved policy document, Governing Body. syesolution

HR records, appointment letters, sorganizational chart

## —–10 8. Review & Monitoring

- Policy will be reviewed annually by the Internationalisation Committee.

- Feedback from students, faculty, and partners will be used for improvements.

## Integration into Policy/Practice

- Student, faculty, and partner feedback will be systematically collected through surveys, focus groups, and direct consultations.

- Feedback will be documented in structured reports and reviewed periodically by the International Relations/Exchange Office.

- **A feedback-action loop will be maintained:** collection → analysis → corrective measures → communication back to stakeholders.

## Student Feedback

- Focus on academic quality, cultural experience, support services, housing, and affordability.

- Captured via post-exchange surveys and end-of–semester review forms.

- Used to refine orientation programs, improve partner selection, and strengthen campus support.

## Faculty Feedback

- Focus on teaching experience abroad, research collaboration, workload balance, and institutional support.

- Collected via faculty debrief reports and structured feedback forms after exchange or collaboration.

- Helps improve faculty mobility policies, joint research opportunities, and institutional frameworks.

## Partner Institution Feedback

- Feedback from international collaborators regarding student preparedness, academic standards, and institutional cooperation.

- Collected through joint review meetings, MoU renewal discussions, and shared reports.

- Used to strengthen long-term partnerships, address challenges, and create mutually beneficial initiatives.

## Improvement Mechanisms

- Annual review meeting to discuss feedback outcomes.

- Continuous update of policies and exchange guidelines based on evidence from feedback.

- Transparent communication to stakeholders on implemented improvements (newsletter, website updates, reports).

# 115. International Student/Relations/Exchange Office

## 1. Indicator Overview

- **Code:** 1S4

- **Max Points:** 10

- **Definition:** Evaluates whether the institution has a dedicated office to manage international relations, exchanges, and student services.

- **Two Components (5 points each):**

**1. Policies & Frameworks:** Existence of formally approved policies governing the establishment and functioning of the office.

**2. Full-time Staff:** Appointment of permanent/contractual employees dedicated to international office functions.

## 2. Purpose

- Ensures structured management of international collaborations.

- Provides a support system for inbound and outbound students & faculty.

- Facilitates student exchange, dual degree programs, joint research, and cultural integration.

- Strengthens the institution’s global visibility and credibility.

## 3. Scoring Logic

Condition Points

Policy only (approved internationalisation policy but no full-time staff) 5

Full-time staff only (office functional but without a formal policy) 5

Both policy & full-time staff present 10

Neither present 0

## 4. Evidence Requirements

- Copy of the approved Internationalisation Policy / International Relations Handbook.

- **Policy document must cover:**

  - Vision for global partnerships.

  - Guidelines for student & faculty mobility.

  - Admission and support mechanisms for international students.

  - Cultural integration and language support.

- HR records / organizational structure showing full-time staff in the International

Office.

- Office website or student handbook showing contact details and services.

## 5. Example Scenarios

## Case 1–Only Policy in Place

- Institution has a documented Internationalisation Policy.

- No dedicated staff, handled by general administration.

- Score = 5/10

## Case 2 – Staff Present Without Policy

- International Office has 3 staff members.

- Policy document not formally approved.

- Score = 5/10

## Case 3 — Policy + Full-time Staff

- Policy approved by governing body.

- Office has a Director + 2 Coordinators + 1 Admin staff.

- Score =10/10

## Case 4 — No Office / Informal Arrangements

- No policy or staff, only ad hoc MoUs.

- Score = 0/10

## 6. Best Practices to Maximise Score

- Draft a comprehensive internationalisation policy aligned with QS I-GAUGE guidelines.

- **Hire dedicated staff:** Director, Exchange Coordinator, Visa Support Officer, Cultural Integration Officer.

- Maintain a visible office space and online portal for international students.

- Track inbound/outbound mobility data through the office.

# 116. International Collaborations

## 1. Indicator Overview

- **Code:** IS5

- **Max Points:** 20

- **Definition:** Rewards institutions for active and outcome–oriented international collaborations.

- **Focus:** Only MoUs/MoAs signed in the last 24 months count, and only if they lead to tangible academic/research activities.

## 2. Sub-components & Scoring (5 points each)

Sub-Indicator Definition

\(i\) Joint

Conferences

\(ii\) Joint Research

(iif) Dual Degrees / Semester Exchange

\(iv\) Summer/ Winter

Schools

MoUs that resulted in international academic conferences, symposiums, or workshops co-hosted.

Collaborative research projects, publications, or funded studies between partner institutions.

Agreements that allow students to pursue dual/joint degrees, or semester-long exchange programs.

Seasonal academic/cultural programs jointly conducted with international partners.

Evidence Required

## MoU/MoA, student

- xchange records, Stranscripts, program brochures.

Program schedules, attendance lists, course 5 material, MoU copy.

- Max Score = 20 (5 × 4 categories)

- Partial credit possible (e.g., if only 2 categories are active → 10/20).

## 3. Purpose & Rationale

- Encourages substantive, not symbolic, international MoUs.

- **Ensures partnerships lead to measurable outcomes:** research, academic mobility, conferences, and training.

- Builds student and faculty exposure to global networks.

- Demonstrates the institution’s global footprint.

## 4. Evidence Requirements

- Signed MoUs/MoAs with date (within last 24 months).

- Reports showing outcomes (joint conference proceedings, research outputs, exchange participation).

- Institutional calendar/website links showcasing events/programs.

- Testimonials or student/faculty participation records.

## 5. Example Scenarios

## Case 1 – Strong Internationalisation

- MoUs with 3 universities.

- Joint conference conducted (v’), joint research ongoing (v’), semester exchange (v’), summer school (v').

- Score = 20/20

## Case 2 — Partial Engagement

- MoU with 2 universities.

- Joint research project ongoing, semester exchange conducted.

- No conferences or summer school.

- Score = 10/20

## Case 3 — Symbolic MoUs Only

- Institution signed MoUs but no tangible outcomes.

- Score = 0/20 (because outcomes, not MoUs, are counted).

## 6. Best Practices to Maximise Score

- Ensure every MoU has measurable deliverables with timelines.

- Create an International Collaboration Committee to monitor outcomes.

- Track and report student/faculty participation regularly.

- Focus on multi-year collaborations with built-in research/publication outputs.

- Diversify partners across regions (Asia, EU, US, Africa) to strengthen credibility.
